### Post #149: 2025-09-12 21:34 `[Gain]` `[Gain]`

**I Made $0 in 9 Years of Trading (And Why That’s Actually a Win)**

(Inspired by my brotha from another motha @Bobdog ‘s click bait style post :) ) 

So here’s the thing that might make you laugh, cry, or pissed: After nearly a decade of trading, investing, and what I can only describe as “financial gambling with extra steps,” my portfolio is sitting at a whopping $0.00% return.

Not $0. Zero *percent*.

Meaning I’ve essentially been running in place for nine years while the market did… well, whatever the hell markets do.

The Journey to Nowhere (But Everywhere?)
Picture this: It’s April 2016. Obama’s still president, everyone’s playing Pokémon GO, and I’m convinced I’m about to become the next Warren Buffett. Spoiler alert: I was not.

What followed was nine years of what I lovingly call “expensive education.” I joined every WhatsApp group known to mankind (seriously, there were THOUSANDS of them). Discord servers, StockTwits groups, Telegram channels, email newsletters – if someone was promising to make me rich, I was there with my credit card ready.

I spent so much money on these “exclusive” trading groups that I probably could’ve bought a decent car. Or a small house. Or, you know, just invested in an index fund and called it a day.

The Highs and Lows (Mostly Lows)
The crazy part? I actually had some wins along the way. Made $100K in a single YOLO call once. Felt like a genius for approximately 24 hours. Then promptly lost $200K on another “sure thing” YOLO call because, apparently, I’m a slow learner.

It was like being addicted to something, except instead of substances, I was mainlining market volatility and hopium. Every salary, every bonus, every bit of savings – straight into the trading account. My bank account was basically a funnel to the stock market.

The Plot Twist: Getting Sober
Then 2025 happened. Something clicked. Maybe it was hitting rock bottom, maybe it was finally growing a brain cell, but I decided to go “sober” from trading addiction.

I unfollowed 99% of the trading gurus, left most of the groups, and did something revolutionary: I joined what I call “theta daddies” – basically the most boring investing community ever. These people talk about things like “time decay” and “consistent returns” instead of diamond hands and rocket ships.

The Anti-Climactic Victory
And here we are. Portfolio value: $452,917.77.
Total return: $0.00 (0.00%)
Time invested: 9 years of my life I’ll never get back

But here’s the weird part – I’m actually celebrating this. Because you know what zero percent means? It means I didn’t lose everything. In a world where most day traders blow up their accounts and disappear into financial obscurity, I somehow managed to tread water for nearly a decade.

I’m like the investing equivalent of someone who went to Las Vegas for nine years straight and came home with exactly the same amount of money they started with. Mathematically impressive in the worst possible way.

The Real Talk
Look, this might inspire some people and absolutely trigger others who’ve actually made money (looking at you, crypto millionaires of 2021). But here’s what I learned:
The market doesn’t care about your mortgage, your dreams, or how many trading courses you’ve bought. It’s not personal, even though it feels personal when you’re watching your account balance swing like a yo-yo operated by a hyperactive toddler.

Sometimes the biggest win is just not losing. Sometimes staying flat is better than going bust. And sometimes admitting you’ve been gambling instead of investing is the first step toward actually building wealth.

What Now?
These days, I follow very few groups to keep my gambling addiction in check (thanks, theta daddies). I’ve learned that boring is beautiful, and that getting rich slowly is infinitely better than getting poor quickly.

Will I ever make actual money investing? Probably. Will it be through diamond hands and YOLO calls? Definitely not. Will I look back on these nine years as the most expensive education ever? Absolutely.

But hey, at least I’ve got one hell of a story to tell. And a portfolio that’s perfectly, mathematically, hilariously flat.

Zero percent, baby. I’m basically the human equivalent of a savings account.

P.S. - If you’re thinking about joining 47 different trading Discord servers and following every “guru” on Twitter, maybe just buy an index fund instead. Your future self will thank you. Or at least not want to time travel back and slap you.

Thanks 🙏🏽 to @average_advisor @Bobdog and @Rise Theta daddies and members who I consider as family and home. Even though I am not interacting as much nowadays with them (work/baby priorities) family will always remain family. Also I am fly on the wall on their forum chats all day from the bathroom and all evening and nights from rest of the house. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/RM20252029/ZXJ/i-made-dollar0-in-9-years-of-t*

### Post #153: 2025-09-14 22:10 `[News]`

**🚨 Wall Street's Hidden Time Bomb: What Every Investor Should Know**

Hey everyone! 👋 I wanted to break down some crucial market insights that could seriously impact your investments. While everyone's talking about Trump's tariffs, there's a much bigger storm brewing...

 📈 The Current Market Euphoria
Right now, the stock market looks AMAZING:
- S&P 500, Dow Jones, and Nasdaq all hit record highs on September 11th
- AI hype is driving massive optimism (we're talking a potential $15.7 trillion opportunity by 2030!)
- Everyone's betting on Fed rate cuts

But here's the thing about markets: when things seem too good to be true, they usually are.

💰 Trump's Tariff Impact (The Obvious Concern)
Let's talk about what's making headlines first:
The Numbers:
- Trump announced 10% global base tariffs back in April
- Since then, inflation jumped from 2.35% to 2.92% in just 3 months
- The market initially TANKED when tariffs were announced (S&P 500's 5th steepest two-day drop since 1950!)

The Problem:
According to New York Fed economists, Trump's tariffs are creating a messy situation where:
- Input costs for U.S. manufacturers are rising
- This pushes inflation higher
- Companies pass costs to consumers

Plot twist: The Supreme Court will decide in November if these tariffs are even legal!

💣 The REAL Time Bomb (What Wall Street Insiders Are Worried About)

Here's what the pros are actually losing sleep over: VALUATIONS

Meet the Shiller P/E Ratio (Your New Best Friend)
Think of this as the market's "reality check" tool:
- It averages stock prices against 10 years of earnings (smooths out the noise)
- Historical average since 1871: 17.28
- Current reading: 39.58 😱

Why This Should Terrify You
This is the 3rd highest valuation in 154 years of market history! The only times it was higher:
1. January 2022 (right before the crash)
2. December 1999 (right before the dot-com bubble burst)

Historical Reality Check
Every time the Shiller P/E topped 30 for 2+ months, here's what happened next:
- Market crashes of 20% to 89%
- Yes, 89% during the Great Depression
- Even "normal" crashes were 50%+

⏰ Timing Is Everything (But Also Impossible)

The Bad News: Nobody can predict WHEN this will happen
The Good News: History shows bear markets are short-lived

Bear Market Facts:
- Average length: 286 days (less than 10 months)
- Bull markets last 3.5x longer on average
- Long-term investors always win

🎯 What This Means for YOU

If you're a short-term trader: Be extremely careful. We're in dangerous territory.

If you're a long-term investor: Stay the course, but maybe don't go all-in right now.

Smart moves to consider:
- Keep some cash on the sidelines
- Don't panic when (not if) the correction comes
- Remember: every major crash has been followed by recovery
- Dollar-cost averaging becomes your best friend

🔮 The Bottom Line
While everyone's distracted by tariff drama, the real elephant in the room is that stocks are historically expensive. This doesn't mean sell everything tomorrow, but it DOES mean:

1. Be prepared for volatility
2. Don't chase the current rally
3. Keep perspective - crashes create opportunities
4. Stay invested long-term (but maybe not at these levels)

The market is like that friend who's had too much to drink at the party - they might keep dancing for a while, but eventually, reality hits.

What do you think? Are you adjusting your strategy based on these valuations? Drop your thoughts in the comments!** 👇

Remember: This is educational content, not financial advice. Always do your own research and consider consulting with a financial advisor.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/RM20252029/Z3V/wall-streets-hidden-time-bomb-*

### Post #157: 2025-09-20 03:11 — `$LYFT` `[Dd]`

**$LYFT ANALYSIS: The Waymo Rally Reality Check**

THE SETUP:
$LYFT is having a moment - up +87% over 6 months with the latest pump coming from their Waymo robotaxi partnership announcement for Nashville 2026. But before you FOMO in, let's break down why this might be a classic "buy the rumor, sell the news" scenario.

THE CATALYST:
✅ Waymo partnership for Nashville robotaxi launch in 2026
✅ Lyft's Flexdrive will handle fleet management
✅ Potential for higher utilization rates and improved unit economics
✅ Brand credibility boost from partnering with autonomy leader

THE REALITY CHECK:
The competitive gap with Uber is MASSIVE and that's not changing overnight:
LYFT vs UBER - The Numbers Don't Lie:

Active Users: 26.1M vs 180M (7x difference!)
Q2 Bookings: $4.49B vs $46.8B (10x difference!)
EBITDA Margin: 2.9% vs 4.5%
Forward P/E: ~18x vs ~33x

WHY I'M STAYING NEUTRAL:
🔴 Scale Disadvantage: Uber's global flywheel gives them pricing power and better economics
🔴 Execution Risk: 2026 is far away - lots can go wrong
🔴 Valuation Concerns: Stock up 87%, analysts see 16% downside to $18.64 PT
🔴 Margin Pressure: Competition with Uber consistently hurts profitability
🟡 The Bull Case: If the Waymo integration works perfectly, Lyft could see improved utilization and brand perception. The Nashville facility build-out shows commitment to AV infrastructure.

MY TAKE:
This partnership is smart and practical, but it doesn't instantly erase Uber's massive scale advantages. With the stock already up 87% and analysts calling for downside, the good news might already be priced in.

Wall Street Consensus: HOLD rating with significant downside risk

Strategy: Wait for a pullback or better entry point. The long-term AV story is compelling, but execution risk is high and valuation looks stretched here.

### Post #166: 2025-10-10 02:25 — `$INTC` `[Dd]`

**$INTC : The Rally Everyone's Talking About. But Should You Be Worried?**

Intel just pulled off something incredible - a 60% surge since August after landing three massive deals:
• $11.1B US government equity stake
• $5B investment from Nvidia (buying 4% of Intel)
• $2B from SoftBank

The stock doubled from its 2024 low of $17 to nearly $38 today. Sounds amazing, right?

But here's where it gets interesting...

HSBC analyst Frank Lee just dropped a bombshell - he downgraded Intel from HOLD to SELL, calling it "time to bail out." His price target? $24 per share. That's a potential 36% drop from current levels.

Why the bearish call despite all the good news?

1. The foundry business is still broken - Recent cancellation of 18A offerings for external clients raised more red flags about execution

2. The Nvidia deal has limitations - Sure, Nvidia will buy x86 CPUs from Intel, but they've made it clear their long-term roadmap centers on ARM-based chips. Limited upside visibility here.

3. The TSMC partnership dream is unlikely - Lee says the only deal that could truly change the game would involve tech-sharing with TSMC. But TSMC has over $100B committed to US expansion projects. Why would they share crown-jewel technology with a rival?

4. Wall Street agrees it's overcooked - Consensus rating: HOLD (26 Holds, 5 Sells, just 2 Buys). Average price target: $26.48 - that's a 30% drop from here.

The bottom line: Intel's rally appears "narrative-driven" while fundamentals remain under pressure. The re-rating may be overdone.

Here's my question for you traders out there:

Are you holding Intel right now? Did you ride this 60% rally, or are you sitting on the sidelines?

Drop a comment:
🟢 if you're BULLISH and staying in
🔴 if you're BEARISH and taking profits
🟡 if you're UNSURE and want to hear more perspectives

Let's discuss - what's your take? Is this just analyst noise, or is Frank Lee onto something? The fundamentals vs narrative battle is real here.

### Post #168: 2025-10-11 19:53 `[Discuss]`

**TRADERS, BUCKLE UP - This Week Could Get WILD**

Just finished analyzing what's coming at us next week and honestly, we might be heading into one of the most volatile weeks of 2025. Here's what's about to hit the markets:

THE BIG 5 FACTORS:

1. Trump just dropped a NUCLEAR tariff bomb on China
We're talking 100% additional tariffs announced Friday. That brings total US tariffs on Chinese goods to 130%. This is in response to China restricting rare earth mineral exports (the stuff that powers your iPhone and Tesla). Markets already shed 900 points on Friday alone. China hasn't officially responded yet, but you know it's coming.

2. Government shutdown is getting serious
We're 10 days into the shutdown now. 900,000 federal workers furloughed, another 700,000 working without pay. Treasury and Health departments just got hit with 4,000+ layoff notices. This isn't just political theater anymore - it's affecting real economic function.

3. Earnings season kicks off HARD next week
JPMorgan Chase, Citigroup, Wells Fargo, Goldman Sachs, Bank of America - they're all reporting. Analysts expect 8% year-over-year growth for the S&P 500, but here's the question: will the guidance reflect confidence or caution given everything else happening?

4. We're flying blind on economic data
No jobs report. No CPI data. Bureau of Labor Statistics and Census Bureau are shut down. We're supposed to get retail sales, PPI, and industrial production numbers next week, but who knows if that's actually happening. Trading without economic indicators is like driving with your eyes closed.

5. Crypto got absolutely demolished
19 billion dollars in leveraged positions liquidated this week. Bitcoin dropped 8%, touching 102K before bouncing back to 113K. XRP crashed over 22%. The correlation between traditional markets and crypto is stronger than ever - when Wall Street sneezes, crypto catches pneumonia.

MY TAKE:
Next week's bank earnings will be the real tell. If they come in strong with confident guidance, we might see some stabilization. But if they express concerns about consumer spending or corporate outlooks given the trade war and shutdown, we could see another leg down.

QUESTION FOR YOU:
Are you going defensive, buying the dip, or sitting in cash right now?

### Post #169: 2025-10-12 14:34 `[Discuss]`

**TRADERS, WE NEED TO TALK ABOUT THE AI BUBBLE 🎯**

The Bank of England just dropped a warning that should make every trader pay attention. Let me break down what's really happening with AI valuations right now.

OpenAI is valued at $500 billion. Read that again. Half a trillion dollars for a company that LOST $7.8 billion in the first half of 2025 alone. They're not even close to profitable and reportedly need 10x their current revenue just to break even.

Here's where it gets interesting: Anthropic has nearly tripled its valuation. The big four (Meta, Alphabet, Microsoft, Amazon) are spending the equivalent of Portugal's ENTIRE GDP on AI infrastructure this year. That's not normal spending, that's bubble behavior.

But here's the kicker - there's circular financing happening. Nvidia invests in OpenAI, OpenAI buys Nvidia chips. Sound familiar? This kind of creative accounting kept things looking good in 2000 too, right before the dotcom crash.

So what could actually pop this bubble?

The same thing that triggered the dotcom collapse in March 2000 - one credible article questioning profitability. That's all it took back then. Everyday investors started selling, and the whole thing unraveled.

Right now, investors aren't betting on current profits. They're betting on artificial superintelligence that could be worth $14 quadrillion (yes, with a Q). They're betting on a future where AI does everything humans can do, but better.

The question you need to ask yourself: Are you investing in reality or hope?

I'm not saying AI won't change the world. The internet wasn't a fad, even though the dotcom bubble burst. But bubbles are EXTREMELY disruptive. Stocks fall. Pensions suffer. Unemployment rises. Real potential gets crowded out by hype.

Here's my take for your portfolios: The valuations are stretched. When regular investors start fearing they've made a mistake, they'll sell. And that fear can spread fast.

Question for you all - what's your position on AI stocks right now? Are you holding, buying more, or starting to exit? Drop your thoughts below because I genuinely want to know what you're all thinking.

And here's the real question: If OpenAI announced tomorrow they need another 5 years to profitability, would you still hold?

Let's discuss. I'm reading every comment.

### Post #171: 2025-10-16 03:50 — `$XOM` `[Dd]`

**$XOM EXXON MOBIL: Is This the Setup Everyone's Missing?**

EXXON MOBIL: Is This the Setup Everyone's Missing?

Here's something that caught my attention today and I want your take on it.

$XOM stock is trading at the same levels we saw back in late 2022. Meanwhile, oil prices have crashed back to early 2021 territory with WTI around $58 and Brent at $62.

But here's where it gets interesting...

THE SETUP:
While the stock is stuck in neutral and oil is getting hammered (IEA is calling for oversupply into 2026), Exxon's fundamentals are actually STRONGER than they were during the last cycle:
Net debt to EBITDA is sitting at just 0.4x (that's fortress-level for an oil major)
They've paid down debt to an 8% net-debt-to-capital ratio
Still generating serious cash flow even at these depressed oil prices

THE AGGRESSIVE MOVE:
Here's what really stands out: their buyback program is relentless. Check this progression:
2021: $0.15 billion in buybacks (basically nothing)
2022: $14.9 billion
2023: $17.4 billion
2024: Nearly $20 billion
2025 YTD: Already $9.8 billion bought back
They've committed to $20 billion per year through 2026. That's serious capital returning to shareholders while simultaneously deleveraging.

THE MATH:
Analysts are expecting 2025 EPS to drop to $6.79 (down 12.8% year-over-year) because of these oil prices. BUT, they're projecting a rebound to $7.41 in 2026 if crude stabilizes.
Current valuation: 16.5x 2025 earnings, dropping to 15.2x 2026 earnings
Wall Street consensus: 11 Buy ratings, 7 Hold ratings, ZERO Sell ratings
Average price target: $126.65 (that's about 14% upside from current levels)

THE QUESTION:
Even traders who aren't oil bulls are saying prices in the $50s won't stick. At some point, these levels make marginal production uneconomic, which historically forces OPEC+ and producers to pull back supply.
If oil just moves to the mid-$60s (not even back to $80-90), the math on XOM changes fast because:

Lower debt means more free cash flow goes straight to buybacks
Fewer shares outstanding means EPS accelerates
Clean balance sheet means they can maintain this through volatility

MY TAKE:
This looks like a classic "hated trade" setup. Everyone's focused on the oil supply glut, but nobody's talking about how much stronger XOM's position is compared to previous cycles.

YOUR TURN:
What's your read on this? Are you:
A) Buying this dip while oil is depressed
B) Waiting for oil to confirm a bottom first
C) Staying away from energy completely
D) Already positioned and adding more

Drop a comment with your letter and reasoning. 

Curious to see where everyone stands on this one.
And if you're in energy stocks, what's your current play? XOM, CVX, or something else entirely?

For those who don't know, Ives is a 5-star analyst at Wedbush covering Big Tech. When he talks, institutions listen. And right now, he's calling for tech stocks to surge another 10%+ into year-end.

But here's what caught my attention: He's explicitly saying this is a 1996 moment, NOT a 1999 bubble.
That's a massive statement. Let me break down what he means and his three top picks.

The 1996 vs 1999 Debate:
Everyone's nervous right now. The top 10 US companies control nearly 40% of the S&P 500 market cap. Some growth stocks are trading at 50x+ forward earnings. This looks exactly like the dot-com peak, right?
Wrong, according to Ives.

In 1999, companies had no revenue, no business model, just dreams and websites. In 2025, we have real revenue, proven AI use cases, and an actual technological revolution happening. The Fourth Industrial Revolution isn't hype, it's here.

His Three Conviction Buys:

$AAPL  - Apple: The Hidden AI Giant

Current Price: $247.45 Ives Target: $310 (25% upside) Consensus: Moderate Buy

The Thesis: Apple has 2.4 billion iOS devices and 1.5 billion iPhones worldwide. That's the largest consumer installed base on the planet. Yet ZERO AI premium is priced into the stock right now.

Apple Intelligence is already built into iOS 18, iPadOS 18, and macOS Sequoia, but the market isn't giving them credit yet. Ives believes AI monetization alone could add $75-$100 per share over the next few years.

Last quarter delivered $94 billion in revenue, up 10% year-over-year and beating forecasts by nearly $5 billion. EPS came in at $1.57, beating by 14 cents and up 12% year-over-year.

The play here: You're buying the world's strongest ecosystem at a discount because the AI story hasn't been fully told yet.

$TSLA  - Tesla: The AI Powerhouse Disguised as a Car Company

Current Price: $428.75 Ives Target: $600 (40% upside) Consensus: Hold (but Ives is aggressively bullish)

The Controversial Take: Automotive revenue dropped 16% year-over-year last quarter. The car business is struggling. So why is Ives predicting Tesla hits $2 trillion market cap by early 2026 and $3 trillion by end of 2026?
Because Tesla isn't a car company anymore. It's an AI and robotics company that happens to make cars.
FSD and Robotaxi: Launched in Austin in June, expanded to San Francisco Bay Area, with testing licenses secured in Arizona and Nevada. Expansion planned for Colorado and Illinois. This is real, operational autonomous driving generating revenue today.

Optimus Robot Program: Musk predicts Optimus could eventually account for 80% of Tesla's revenue. That sounds insane until you consider what a humanoid robot capable of doing manual labor, working in toxic environments, and operating 24/7 without breaks is actually worth to industries worldwide.

The Risk: Execution. Musk has missed timelines before. Supply chain issues with China are slowing production. But if even half of this vision plays out, current valuations look cheap.

$CRM  - Salesforce: The Enterprise AI Dark Horse

Current Price: $246 Ives Target: $375 (52.5% upside) Consensus: Moderate Buy with average target at $329.86 (34% upside)

The Agentforce Revolution: Salesforce isn't just CRM software anymore. They've evolved into a full enterprise AI platform with Agentforce, their new agentic AI suite that can handle customer service, sales, and workflow tasks autonomously.

This month they announced major partnerships:
* OpenAI integration allowing Agentforce 360 access through ChatGPT
* Google partnership linking Gemini models with Agentforce 360

Last quarter: $10.2 billion revenue (up 10% year-over-year), beating expectations. EPS of $2.91, crushing forecasts by 13 cents.

The Target: $60 billion in annual revenue by FY2030. That's nearly 6x current levels.
Ives' key insight: 40% of work at Fortune 1000 companies will be elevated by AI by 2029. Salesforce is positioning to capture that wave with 20k paying Agentforce customers expected by fiscal year-end 2026.

Why This Matters Now:
We're at an inflection point. The companies building real AI infrastructure with paying customers are separating from the pretenders. Apple has the distribution, Tesla has the vision, and Salesforce has the enterprise relationships.
All three are already generating massive revenue and have clear paths to AI monetization. This isn't speculation, it's execution.

The Contrarian Angles:

AAPL: Consensus only sees 3% upside vs Ives' 25%. Someone's very wrong here.

TSLA: Consensus is actually bearish with average target implying 15% downside. Ives is on an island calling for 40% upside. High conviction or high risk?

CRM: This is the safest play. Even conservative analysts see 34% upside. Ives sees 52.5%. Less controversy, solid execution.

My Take:
If Ives is right about this being 1996 and not 1999, we're not at the end of the tech rally, we're in the middle innings. The difference is these companies have:
* Real revenue and profits
* Massive installed bases
* Clear AI monetization paths
* Proven execution ability

But if he's wrong and this is 1999, these valuations will get destroyed when sentiment shifts.

### Post #176: 2025-10-19 15:56 — `$RGTI, $AMZN, $QBTS` `[Dd]`

**While everyone's fighting over AI stocks, smart money is quietly positioning in the NEXT revolution $RGTI $QBTS $AMZN**

Quantum computing is no longer science fiction. It's happening right now, and these three stocks are leading the charge.

Why Quantum Computing Changes Everything:
Traditional computers process information in bits (0s and 1s). Quantum computers use qubits that can be both 0 AND 1 simultaneously. This means they can solve complex problems exponentially faster than anything we have today.

Think about it: AI revolutionized how we process data. Quantum computing will revolutionize how we solve problems that AI can't even touch yet.

The Three Plays with Very Positive Investor Sentiment:

RGTI - Rigetti Computing The Pure Play Innovation Story
Just achieved 99.5% gate fidelity with their new 36-qubit modular system. That's cutting error rates in HALF compared to previous models. This is massive because error correction has been quantum computing's biggest barrier.
They're targeting 100+ qubits by year-end and planning 1,000+ qubit systems for commercial use. Expanding their cloud platform to make quantum accessible to businesses.
Analyst consensus: Strong Buy Average price target: $28.10 Investor sentiment: Very Positive
The catch: Price target implies 40% downside from current levels. This one's for believers in the technology, not short-term traders.

QBTS - D-Wave Quantum The Scale Player
Just launched their sixth-generation Advantage2 system with over 4,400 qubits. That's not a typo. Four thousand, four hundred qubits. Most advanced and energy-efficient quantum computer they've ever built.
Secured $400 million in fresh funding to scale operations. Expanding globally with new partnerships in Europe and Asia. This is a company preparing for commercialization, not just research.
Analyst consensus: Strong Buy Average price target: $27.18 (implies 28.62% downside) Investor sentiment: Very Positive

AMZN - Amazon The Sleeping Giant
Released their first quantum chip, Ocelot, earlier this year. Designed to simplify error correction and reduce hardware overhead. When Amazon enters a space, they usually dominate it eventually.
This is the safest play of the three. You're buying Amazon with quantum computing as a free option. If quantum takes off, AMZN gets a massive boost. If it doesn't, you still own one of the best companies in the world.
Analyst consensus: Strong Buy Average price target: $267.30 (implies 25.55% UPSIDE) Investor sentiment: Positive

The Investment Thesis:

Quantum computing is where AI was 5 years ago. Early, speculative, but with breakthrough potential that could create trillion-dollar markets.
The technology can solve problems in drug discovery, financial modeling, cryptography, and optimization that current computers literally cannot handle. We're talking about capabilities that don't exist today.

The Risk Factor:
Let's be honest: This is extremely speculative. Both RGTI and QBTS have price targets BELOW current levels. The market is pricing in massive future growth that may not materialize. These companies are burning cash, and commercialization is still years away.
If quantum computing doesn't scale as expected, or if it takes longer than anticipated, these stocks could get crushed.
But here's my thought: Every revolutionary technology looked too expensive before it changed the world.

My Personal Take:

I'm watching all three, but if I had to rank them:
1. AMZN - Lowest risk, still has upside, quantum is just a bonus
2. QBTS - Most advanced system, fresh capital, global expansion
3. RGTI - Highest risk/reward, pure play on the technology

The Two Questions I Want Your Opinion On:
1. Do you think quantum computing will be the next AI-level revolution, or is it overhyped?
2. Would you rather own the pure plays (RGTI, QBTS) with higher risk/reward, or the safer bet (AMZN) with quantum exposure as a bonus?

Drop your thoughts below. I'm genuinely curious where the community stands on this.

### Post #177: 2025-10-19 21:52 `[Dd]`

**$BRK.B is 9% off its highs and nobody's talking about it.**

While everyone's chasing AI stocks and speculative plays, one of the most bulletproof companies in America is sitting at a discount. And the reason it's down might actually be the reason to buy.

Let me explain.

The Setup:

Berkshire Hathaway is trading at $491, sitting 9% below its 52-week high while the broader market keeps pushing to new records. This is rare. Quality businesses with Berkshire's track record don't go on sale often.

Two headlines are weighing on the stock:
1. Warren Buffett announced he's stepping down as CEO by year-end. Greg Abel is taking over.
2. Last quarter was messy. They took a $5 billion impairment on Kraft Heinz (their second one), revenue dropped 1.2%, and operating income fell 3.8%.

The market sees uncertainty and weakness. I see opportunity.

Why The Sell-Off Is Overdone:

Let's start with what everyone's worried about: Buffett retiring.

Yes, it's the end of an era. But here's what people forget: Greg Abel has been groomed for this role for years. He's been running Berkshire Hathaway Energy and has Buffett's full confidence. The succession plan is clear, deliberate, and probably better executed than 99% of CEO transitions.

And honestly, at some point, you have to separate the man from the machine. Berkshire's competitive advantages don't disappear when Buffett retires:
* Diversified operating businesses generating steady cash flow
* Massive insurance float providing capital for investments
* Fortress balance sheet with unprecedented optionality
* Culture of disciplined capital allocation

Now let's talk about Q2, which looked rough on the surface but has some interesting details underneath.

The Good News Nobody's Mentioning:

BNSF Railway: Profit jumped from $1.23B to $1.47B year-over-year. That's a 19.5% increase.

Berkshire Hathaway Energy: Profit grew from $655M to $702M. That's 7.2% growth.

Insurance Float: Now at $174 billion, up from last year, generating robust investment income from Treasury holdings.

The private operating businesses are doing extremely well. They're just getting overshadowed by the Kraft Heinz impairment, which, let's be honest, was already priced into everyone's expectations.

The OxyChem Acquisition:
A few days ago, Berkshire agreed to buy OxyChem from Occidental for $9.7 billion in cash. This is exactly what long-term investors wanted to see.

It's not a flashy tech acquisition, but it's a durable, cash-generating industrial asset that fits perfectly into Berkshire's playbook. High-quality earnings, stable cash flows, and it barely moves the needle for a company this size, which means there's room for more deals.

The $340 Billion War Chest:

Here's the part that gets me excited. Berkshire is sitting on roughly $340 billion in cash and Treasury bills. They are literally the largest private holder of U.S. Treasury bills.

Think about what that means:
* Generating meaningful income right now from T-bills
* Massive optionality if markets correct and deals get cheaper
* Zero pressure to deploy capital into bad investments
* Ability to act decisively when opportunities arise

In a bear market, this cash pile becomes a weapon. In a bull market, it's generating steady returns while waiting for the right pitch.

The Valuation Case:
Book value per share is estimated at $310. Current price is $491. That's 1.6x book value.

For a company with:
* Best-in-class private businesses compounding earnings
* $340 billion in deployable capital
* Improving rails and utilities
* Recently bolstered industrial footprint
* Quality public holdings (American Express, Bank of America, Coca-Cola, Apple)

Paying 1.6x book is not expensive. It's reasonable, maybe even cheap when you factor in the optionality.

Average analyst price target is $536, implying 10% upside over the next 12 months. But here's the thing: that target doesn't fully account for what happens if Berkshire deploys even a fraction of that cash pile into good acquisitions.

The Contrarian Angle:
The market is pricing in transition risk and near-term earnings weakness. What it's NOT pricing in:
* Greg Abel potentially being more aggressive with acquisitions than Buffett in recent years
* The optionality value of that massive cash position
* The steady compounding of the private operating businesses
* The possibility that lower rates reignite M&A activity, where Berkshire excels

Everyone's worried about what happens after Buffett. I'm thinking about what Greg Abel might do with $340 billion in firepower.

The Risks You Need to Know:

Let me be straight about the downside:
1. Leadership transition could be rougher than expected. Abel has big shoes to fill.
2. If rates fall, T-bill income decreases. That hurts near-term earnings.
3. No buybacks right now means no mechanical support for the stock price.
4. The Kraft Heinz situation could get worse before it gets better.
5. At this size, it's hard to move the needle with acquisitions. They need huge deals.

But here's my counter: These are all known risks. The market has already priced them in with the 9% pullback from highs.

My Honest Take:

This is the most boring buy-the-dip opportunity I've seen in a while. And that's exactly why I like it.

While everyone's speculating on quantum computing, AI chips, and which tech stock will 10x, Berkshire is sitting here with:
* Proven businesses generating steady cash
* The biggest war chest in corporate America
* A reasonable valuation
* A clear succession plan

It's not sexy. It's not going to double in six months. But it's high-quality, low-drama compounding with a margin of safety.

For long-term investors who want exposure to quality with upside optionality, this dip looks like a gift.

Three Questions for the Community:
1. Are you more worried about Buffett retiring, or excited about what Greg Abel might do with $340 billion?
2. Would you rather own Berkshire at 1.6x book with massive optionality, or chase higher-growth tech stocks at nosebleed valuations?
3. If Berkshire deploys half that cash pile into good acquisitions over the next 2-3 years, what do you think happens to the stock price?
 Drop your thoughts. I genuinely want to hear if anyone sees something I'm missing here.

And be honest: Is this too boring for your portfolio, or is boring exactly what you need right now?

### Post #178: 2025-10-20 21:14 — `$NVTS` `[Dd]`

**$NVTS just dropped a bombshell and Wall Street is sleeping on it**

While everyone's obsessed with AI chips, Navitas Semiconductor quietly solved the billion-dollar problem nobody's watching: AI's MASSIVE power consumption crisis.

The Setup: Shares exploded 21% on October 13, then another 26% the next day after announcing breakthrough progress on 800V power tech for Nvidia data centers. That's a 47% move in 48 hours.

Here's Why This Could Run Further:

The Problem They're Solving: AI data centers are hitting a wall. Power consumption is expected to jump from 7 GW in 2023 to over 70 GW by 2030. That's a 10x increase. Traditional 48V systems can't handle it. They're too inefficient and can only support 100-200 kilowatt racks.

The NVTS Solution: Their 800V architecture delivers 98% conversion efficiency and can handle over 1 megawatt per rack. That's 5-10x the power density of current systems. Using gallium nitride and silicon carbide tech they already perfected in consumer electronics, now scaled for AI infrastructure.

The Nvidia Connection: They're partnering directly with Nvidia to build this 800V infrastructure. When Nvidia backs your tech, other data center operators will follow. Think about it - every major AI player needs this solution.
Manufacturing Scale-Up: Just partnered with Powerchip to shift from 6-inch to 8-inch wafers. That's an 80% boost in chip output per wafer. They're preparing for massive demand.

The Contrarian Play: Here's where it gets interesting. Wall Street analysts have an average price target of $8.88, implying 43% downside from current levels. BUT those targets were set before this week's announcements. Once analysts update their models for the 800V breakthrough and Nvidia partnership, expect significant revisions.

The company is still unprofitable, burning cash on R&D and scaling production. But they have $161 million in cash against only $7 million in debt. Runway is solid.

The Risk: This is an early-stage growth story trading at a projected 2028 P/E of 300x. Execution risk is real. If they stumble on commercialization or competitors catch up, this could give back gains fast.

My Take: The 250% YTD rally looks insane on the surface, but the AI power infrastructure market is just getting started. 

If NVTS becomes the standard for 800V data centers, this valuation could actually look cheap in hindsight.

Question for you: Are you more interested in companies building AI chips or companies solving AI's infrastructure problems? Drop your thoughts below.

And here's the real question: Would you rather buy after a 47% surge with catalysts accelerating, or wait for a pullback that might never come?

### Post #182: 2025-10-25 22:02 — `$F` `[Dd]`

**$F AT $13.84: Value Trap or Hidden Gem? Let's Break This Down**

Here's what caught my attention in yesterday's report:

THE NUMBERS THAT MATTER
Net margin jumped from 1.9% to 2.5% - and analysts project it DOUBLING to 3.6% by 2028. Earnings crushed it with 33.4% growth last year after averaging NEGATIVE growth for 5 years. But here's where it gets interesting...

THE VALUATION PUZZLE
Ford trades at 11.7x earnings while the auto industry averages 18.8x. Sounds cheap, right? But DCF fair value says $8.64 while we're sitting at $13.84. That's a 60% premium to intrinsic value.
Stock is already trading 6.4% ABOVE analyst consensus target. Market is pricing in something the analysts aren't seeing yet.

THE BULL CASE
Ford Pro software subscriptions up 24% year-over-year. Aftermarket services now driving almost 20% of division EBIT - these are high-margin recurring revenues. The shift from selling trucks to selling services is real.

Manufacturing efficiencies and cost controls creating sustainable margin expansion. This isn't just a temporary win.

THE BEAR CASE
Revenue forecast: NEGATIVE 0.6% per year for next 3 years. You're betting on margin expansion while top line shrinks. $2 billion annual tariff headwind that could wipe out margin gains. Heavy reliance on trucks and SUVs - if EV transition accelerates faster than Ford adapts, this could get ugly. Warranty and recall costs keep dragging profitability.

MY TAKE
This is a classic momentum vs valuation debate. Bulls are right about operational improvements. Bears are right about revenue headwinds and rich valuation vs fundamentals.

Question: Are you paying for proven results or hoping margin expansion outpaces revenue decline?

**$GME GAMESTOP: When Two Investors Look At The Same Stock And See COMPLETELY Different Realities**

Down 12% in the past month. Down 27.3% year-to-date. But still up 660% over five years.

GME just keeps confusing everyone, and right now there are TWO wildly different valuations floating around - and both camps have legitimate arguments.

THE NUMBERS RIGHT NOW:
* Current Price: Trading around $22.30
* DCF Fair Value: $35.13 (36.5% undervalued)
* P/E Ratio: 27.6x (way above industry avg of 16.7x)
* Valuation Score: 2 out of 6 (not great)
* Free Cash Flow: $474.48M trailing

THE BULL CAMP SAYS: $120 PER SHARE Fair Value: $120 Their View: 81.4% undervalued

Here's their thesis:
* Bitcoin strategy could be a game-changer (literally betting on crypto pivot)
* Sitting on massive cash reserves
* 25% of shares held by retail = army of diamond hands
* Major cost-cutting is working
* Pivoting from dying retail to cash-rich crypto play
* Loyal shareholder base prevents short attacks

THE BEAR CAMP SAYS: $11.91 PER SHARE Fair Value: $11.91 Their View: 87.2% overvalued right now

Here's their counter:
* Revenue is DECLINING despite all the pivots
* Crypto adds risk, not real business value
* Digital transformation hasn't stopped the bleeding
* Stock price driven by memes, not fundamentals
* High volatility makes this a speculation, not investment
* Fierce competition in every direction

CRITICAL CONTEXT: 
The DCF model shows intrinsic value at $35.13 based on free cash flow projections reaching $1.36B by 2035. Growth rates start at 28.6% near-term, then slow to 4.1% long-term.

Q1 2025 showed solid results and the balance sheet is actually strong. But the business model transformation is far from proven.

1. Is the Bitcoin strategy brilliant diversification or desperate distraction?

### Post #190: 2025-11-03 22:37 `[News]`

**STARBUCKS JUST REPORTED EARNINGS - And The Market Is Confused**

Shares popped 1.4% after Thursday's earnings release. CEO says "the turnaround is taking hold." But when you dig into the numbers, this looks like a REALLY expensive bet on a recovery that's far from guaranteed.
Let me break down what actually happened:

THE GOOD NEWS (Finally):

SAME-STORE SALES TURNED POSITIVE After SEVEN straight quarters of declines, Starbucks posted +1% global same-store sales growth. This is huge because it shows the existing stores are growing, not just relying on new locations.

Even better:
* International same-store sales: +3%
* China (8,000 stores): +2%
* U.S. September comps: Turned positive
* Wait times: 80% of U.S. stores now under 4 minutes

The Green Apron initiative (better training + new sequencing algorithm) is actually working.

THE BAD NEWS (This Gets Concerning):

VALUATION IS INSANE
* P/E ratio: 52x (S&P 500 average is 30x)
* The stock is priced like the turnaround already happened
* Operating margin dropped 500 basis points to 9.4%

DIVIDEND WARNING FLAGS
* Payout ratio: 103.9%
* They're paying MORE in dividends than they earn in net income
* Recent dividend "increase" of 1.6% (basically nothing)
* With only $4.5B cash vs $27.9B debt, they can't sustain this
* Dividend cut risk is REAL

THE UGLY TRUTH (Here's What Nobody Is Talking About):

NET INCOME COLLAPSED 85% Down to just $133 million. Let that sink in.

STORE CLOSURES ACCELERATING
* 107 MORE stores closed than opened last quarter
* Over 600 closures in North America alone

THE BILLION-DOLLAR QUESTION They're in the middle of a $1 BILLION restructuring plan, with most costs hitting in FY 2025. So earnings pressure isn't going away anytime soon.

WHAT THE ANALYSTS AREN'T TELLING YOU:
Revenue only grew 5%, but they closed a net 107 stores. So per-store economics might not be improving as much as the headlines suggest.
They still have flat same-store sales in North America (18,000 locations). The growth is coming from international, particularly China, U.K., Japan, and Mexico.

MY REAL QUESTIONS FOR THIS COMMUNITY:
1. Would you buy a stock trading at 52x P/E when earnings just dropped 85%?
2. Is a 1% same-store sales increase really worth celebrating after seven quarters of declines?
3. How long can they maintain a 103.9% payout ratio before cutting the dividend?
4. Are the store closures a sign of smart rationalization or a dying business model?
5. Is CEO Brian Niccol actually turning this around, or is Wall Street giving him too much credit too soon?
6. China showing +2% growth - is that enough to offset North American weakness long-term?

FOR DIVIDEND INVESTORS SPECIFICALLY: That 103.9% payout ratio should terrify you. They can't keep paying more than they earn. With that debt load, a dividend cut might be coming.

So here's my take: The operational improvements are real. But the stock price is pricing in a perfect turnaround when we're still in the early innings with a LOT of pain ahead.

Who here is buying this dip? Who's staying away? And dividend holders - are you worried about that payout ratio or confident they'll protect it?

Let me know what you're seeing that I'm missing.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/RM20252029/8Kg/starbucks-just-reported-earnin*

### Post #195: 2025-11-16 18:13 — `$NVDA, $META, $AAPL, $GOOGL` `[News]`

**WEEKEND TECH BREAKDOWN: 5 Stories That Could Move Your Portfolio Monday $AAPL $NVDA $META $GOOGL**

The tech world just dropped some serious headlines this weekend. 

Let me break down what actually matters for your trades:

APPLE × WECHAT: 

The Silent Money Maker
Apple just secured a 15% revenue cut from WeChat mini-games in China. Jim Cramer is literally questioning why AAPL stock isn't moving on this news. Think about it: WeChat has over a billion users, and Apple is now taking a slice of every in-game purchase. This could be huge recurring revenue that Wall Street is sleeping on.

Question for you: Is the market underpricing Apple's China exposure right now, or is there something Cramer's missing?

NVIDIA: Caught in the Crossfire

Here's where it gets messy. Amazon and Microsoft are backing new legislation (the Gain AI Act) that would restrict Nvidia's AI chip exports to China even further. Yes, you read that right - NVDA's own customers are supporting restrictions on their supplier.

This is pure geopolitics meets business. Nvidia already took a hit from previous export controls, and now the screws are tightening again. But here's the thing: restricted supply to China could mean more chips for US data centers, which might actually help margins.

What's your take? Is this bearish for NVDA short-term, or does domestic AI demand make up for it?

META: Losing Its AI Brain

Yann LeCun, Meta's chief AI scientist, is reportedly leaving to start his own venture. This is the guy who won the Turing Award and has been central to Meta's AI strategy. Zuckerberg has been going all-in on AI to compete with OpenAI and Google, and now he's losing one of his key players.
Talent drain is a real risk in tech. When the top scientists start leaving, it's usually a signal. Could this slow down Meta's AI ambitions right when they need to deliver?

Drop your thoughts: Red flag for META holders, or is this just normal Silicon Valley rotation?

APPLE'S $230 IPHONE POCKET: Brand Power or Too Far?

MKBHD just roasted Apple's new $230 "iPhone Pocket" accessory, calling it a test of blind brand loyalty. I mean, $230 for a pocket. Let that sink in.

But here's the real question: Does Apple have so much brand power that people will literally buy anything with their logo? Or is this where premium pricing finally breaks?

Be honest: Would you buy it? And more importantly, what does this say about Apple's pricing power going forward?

GOOGLE: $6.4B Germany Bet

Alphabet is dropping 5.5 billion euros into German AI infrastructure and data centers. New facility near Frankfurt, massive capacity expansion. This is Google planting a flag in Europe for the AI race.
With EU regulations getting tighter, having local data centers isn't just smart, it's necessary. This positions Google ahead of competitors for European enterprise AI contracts.

Here's my question: Is Europe the next major battleground for AI dominance, and is Google making the right move while others hesitate?

THE BIG PICTURE FOR TRADERS:

Monday's opening could be interesting. We've got Apple with underappreciated China revenue, Nvidia facing political headwinds but potentially stronger domestic demand, Meta losing key talent, and Google expanding aggressively in Europe.
Which of these stories are you watching closest? And which stock are you buying, selling, or holding through this news cycle?

Comment below with your move. Let's see who's reading this situation right.

### Post #196: 2025-11-17 01:51 — `$DIS` `[Dd]`

**$DIS DISNEY: THE MOST POLARIZING STOCK IN MY WATCHLIST RIGHT NOW**

Current price: $108.26 Wall Street target: $140.07 (29% upside) Analyst consensus: STRONG BUY (14 buy, 2 hold, 0 sell)

But here's what nobody is talking about...

THE BRUTAL TRUTH ABOUT DIS
Disney stock is DOWN 21.77% over the past 5 years. Let me repeat that. While the market has been ripping, Disney shareholders have been underwater for half a decade. The Mouse House has been bleeding, and the question everyone should be asking is: Has Bob Iger finally stopped the bleeding, or is this just another dead cat bounce?
Let me break down both sides of this trade because this is NOT a simple buy or sell situation.

THE BULL CASE: Why DIS Could Explode

The $60 Billion Theme Park Bet
Disney just announced a 10-year, $60 billion investment plan for its theme parks and cruise lines. This is the first major expansion effort in nearly a DECADE. They're opening a new park in Abu Dhabi and upgrading all 12 existing parks with VR, interactive tech, and experiences you can't replicate at home.

Disney controls 25% of the global theme park market. That market is projected to grow from $64.6 billion to $82.73 billion by 2032. If they execute this right, theme parks could return as Disney's primary profit engine.

Question: With inflation crushing family budgets, are people really going to pay premium prices for Disney parks, or will they choose cheaper entertainment?

Streaming Finally Turned Profitable
After burning through $11 BILLION trying to compete with Netflix, Disney's streaming platforms (Disney+, Hulu, ESPN+) have finally reached profitability. They're consolidating apps, reducing costs, and the bundled subscription model is working.

The DTC division is projected to become a major profit engine by 2027-2028. If they can maintain subscriber growth while improving margins, this completely changes Disney's valuation story.

But here's my concern: How sticky are these subscribers? When money gets tight, streaming services are the first thing people cut. Can Disney maintain this momentum, or are we looking at another round of subscriber losses?

The Blockbuster Pipeline Is STACKED
Look at what's coming:
* Avatar 3: Fire and Ash (2026) - The first two Avatar films are #1 and #2 all-time worldwide box office
* Avengers: Doomsday (2026) with Robert Downey Jr. as Doctor Doom
* The Mandalorian movie (2026) directed by Jon Favreau
* Avengers: Secret Wars (2028)
* Frozen III, Incredibles 3, Toy Story 5

Inside Out 2 just proved Disney's animation department still prints money. If these tentpole films deliver, we're looking at massive revenue streams for the next 3-5 years.
Counter-argument: Marvel fatigue is REAL. Star Wars has been inconsistent at best. Can Disney actually execute on these franchises, or will they fumble like they did with the recent Marvel TV shows that cost $725 million and flopped?

THE BEAR CASE: Why This Could Be a Value Trap

Bob Iger's Succession Problem
Iger came out of retirement to fix the mess, but he's made it clear he's not staying long-term. Who takes over after him? This is the biggest unknown hanging over Disney. The last successor, Bob Chapek, drove the stock into the ground.

Leadership uncertainty in a company this massive is a red flag. One bad CEO appointment could erase any gains we see in the next 2-3 years.

The Marvel and Star Wars Problems Haven't Been Fully Fixed
Yes, Iger brought back the Daredevil cast and replaced Jonathan Majors with RDJ for the Avengers. But the Star Wars franchise is still a mess. The Acolyte was cancelled after poor performance, and there's still uncertainty about whether they can restore fan confidence.

Disney spent $725 million on Marvel TV shows that mostly disappointed. That's not just a financial loss - it's brand damage. When your core franchises lose their cultural cachet, you're in trouble.

Competition Is Crushing Them
Netflix, Amazon Prime, Apple TV+ - everyone is in the streaming wars now. ESPN is losing sports rights to Amazon and others. The competitive moat that Disney once had is eroding fast.

Even in theme parks, Universal is building competitive experiences. Disney's pricing power might be reaching its limit.

THE PRICE TARGETS THAT MATTER
2025 forecast: $114.62 (5.89% upside) 2026 forecast: $129.14 (19.3% upside) 2028 forecast: $135.60 (25.2% upside) 2030 forecast: $145.80 (34.7% upside)
Wall Street is pricing in steady growth as streaming profits ramp, theme parks reopen fully, and the blockbuster slate delivers. But these targets assume perfect execution - and Disney has NOT been executing perfectly.

MY CONTROVERSIAL TAKE

Disney at $108 is a calculated risk play, not a safe blue-chip hold. If you believe:
* The $60B theme park investment will work
* Streaming can maintain profitability and grow margins
* The blockbuster pipeline will deliver (especially Avatar 3 and the Avengers sequels)
* Iger finds a competent successor
Then this is a multi-year winner with 30-35% upside to 2030.

But if ANY of those pillars crack - bad succession, franchise fatigue, streaming subscriber losses, or failed theme park execution - this stock could easily drift lower for another 5 years.

HERE'S WHAT I WANT TO KNOW FROM YOU
1. Are you buying Disney right now at $108, or waiting for a better entry?
2. Do you trust Bob Iger to fix this mess before he leaves?
3. Which matters more to Disney's future: streaming profits or theme park revenue?
4. Is Marvel fatigue real, or will audiences show up for these new Avengers movies?
5. Would YOU pay Disney's premium prices for parks and streaming right now, or are you cutting back on entertainment spending?

Drop your take below. I want to see who's bullish, who's bearish, and who's sitting this one out completely.

And if you think I'm missing something critical in this analysis, call me out. That's what this community is for.

One more thing: If this breakdown helped you think through the DIS trade differently, smash that like button. The more people engage, the more deep dives like this I'll post.

See you in the comments.

### Post #197: 2025-11-17 04:50 — `$META` `[Dd]`

**$META META STOCK SPLIT BY 2030? This Prediction Just Hit My Desk**

Current price: $609 
Prediction: $1,000+ by 2030 
Required CAGR: Just 9.8%

Here's the wild part: Meta is the ONLY Magnificent Seven stock that has NEVER done a stock split. Not once since its 2012 IPO at $38.

Apple? Split multiple times. 
Amazon? Split. 
Nvidia? Split. 
Tesla? Split twice. 
Google? Split. 
Microsoft? Split multiple times.

Meta? Never touched it.

And now analysts are saying that's about to change. Let me break down why this matters for your portfolio and whether you should care about stock splits at all.

THE CASE FOR A META STOCK SPLIT

First, let's be clear: stock splits don't change the fundamental value of a company. If you own $10,000 worth of Meta stock, you still own $10,000 worth after a split. Period.

BUT here's what happens psychologically and practically:
1. Netflix just announced a 10-for-1 split and the stock jumped on the news alone
2. Retail investors pile in because shares "feel" more affordable
3. Options become more accessible to smaller traders
4. Stock gets more media attention and social media buzz
 Meta peaked at nearly $800 per share. At current levels around $609, it's already expensive for retail. If it hits $1,000 by 2030, we're talking about a stock that would be extremely prohibitive for average investors without fractional shares.

The math to get there? Meta needs a compound annual growth rate of just 9.8% over the next five years. That's not some moonshot prediction - that's actually very achievable given their current trajectory.

Question for you: Do you think stock splits actually matter in an age of fractional share buying, or is this just psychological theater?

WHY META COULD ACTUALLY HIT $1,000

Let's talk about what's driving this prediction, because it's not just hopium.

The AI Advertising Machine Is Printing Money
Meta's Q3 results: Revenue up 26% year-over-year to $51.2 billion. EPS would have climbed 20.2% to $7.25 if you strip out a one-time tax charge.

Their AI-powered ad tools are absolutely destroying it. 

They're using AI to:
* Recommend content that keeps users glued to their platforms
* Help businesses automate and optimize targeted advertising
* Completely automate the ad launch process by end of 2026

This isn't theory. This is already working and generating massive revenue growth RIGHT NOW.

Daily active users across all Meta platforms: 3.54 billion, up 8% year-over-year. They're not losing engagement - they're growing it.

The AI Glasses Bet That Nobody's Talking About
Zuckerberg is going all-in on AI glasses, calling them the "ideal form factor" for AI interaction. His prediction? AI glasses will be mainstream within 10 years.

Think about it: Chatbots can't see or hear what you do. But glasses can. Glasses with AI can interact with the world in real-time while having all the analytical power of chatbots.

If Zuck is even half right about this, Meta's "other revenue" segment could explode by 2030. This is a completely new revenue stream that Wall Street isn't fully pricing in yet.

My take: This sounds crazy until you remember everyone said the same thing about smartphones, tablets, and smartwatches before they became ubiquitous.
Your turn: Do you think AI glasses will actually become mainstream, or is this another Google Glass flop waiting to happen?

The Capex Spend That Spooked Everyone
Here's where it gets controversial. Meta's capital expenditures are going through the roof, and Wall Street hated it. The stock dropped hard after earnings because of increased AI infrastructure spending.

But here's my contrarian view: This capex spend is EXACTLY what you want to see if you're a long-term holder. Meta is building the infrastructure to dominate AI for the next decade. They're not sitting back and letting OpenAI, Google, or Amazon run away with this market.

You know what kills companies? Underinvesting in the future to protect short-term earnings. Meta is doing the opposite.

THE BEAR CASE NOBODY WANTS TO HEAR

Let me play devil's advocate on my own thesis:

That 9.8% CAGR Assumes Perfect Execution
No regulatory headwinds. No competition eating their lunch. No major platform shifts they miss. No recession crushing ad spending. A lot has to go right for five straight years.

The Stock Already Dropped After Earnings
Meta fell significantly post-Q3 despite beating on earnings. The market is clearly concerned about something - whether it's the capex spend, the tax situation, or just valuation concerns.
If the market won't bid this up past $800 even with strong results, what makes us think it'll hit $1,000?

The Magnificent Seven Might Be Overvalued
Let's be honest: The Mag 7 has had an insane run. At some point, valuations matter. Meta's current price already reflects a lot of optimism. Getting to $1,000 means the market has to get even MORE optimistic.

Stock Splits Don't Actually Matter
In the age of fractional shares, who cares if Meta is $600 or $1,000 per share? Robinhood, Fidelity, Schwab - they all offer fractional shares. The "accessibility" argument is increasingly outdated.
So even if Meta hits $1,000, they might not split at all because there's no real business reason to do so anymore.

What's your take: Is the stock split prediction just clickbait, or does it actually signal something meaningful about Meta's growth trajectory?

THE REAL QUESTION YOU SHOULD BE ASKING
Forget the stock split for a second. Here's what actually matters:

Do you believe Meta can grow revenue and earnings at a 10% annual rate for the next five years while maintaining or expanding margins?
If yes, then Meta at $609 is a buy regardless of whether they split or not.

If no, then who cares about a potential split - you shouldn't be buying this stock anyway.
I'm personally more interested in:
* Can they actually make AI glasses work?
* Will regulatory pressure increase or decrease under different political environments?
* Can they maintain their dominance in digital advertising as competition intensifies?
* Is the capex spend going to pay off, or is it dead money?

WHAT I WANT TO KNOW FROM YOU
1. Are you buying META at current levels, or do you think it's overvalued after the recent run?
2. Do you believe Zuckerberg's AI glasses vision, or is this going to be a massive waste of capital?
3. Is the increased capex spending a red flag or a green flag for long-term investors?
4. If Meta hits $1,000 by 2030, do you think they'll actually split, or is that prediction meaningless in the age of fractional shares?
5. Most importantly: Would YOU rather own Meta or Nvidia for the next five years?

Drop your honest take in the comments. I want to see who's bullish, who's bearish, and who thinks this whole stock split prediction is a distraction from the real story.

And if you found value in this breakdown, hit that like button. The algorithm rewards engagement, and the more people see this, the better conversation we'll have in the comments.

Let's debate this one.

Everyone's hunting the next Nvidia, but here's what Wall Street isn't telling you: AI is running out of electricity.

And that's creating the biggest infrastructure opportunity of the decade.

THE BYOP TRADE: BRING YOUR OWN POWER

Four governors just backed a game-changing proposal: new data centers must generate their own energy AND feed it back to the grid.

Meta locked in 100% of a $900M solar project

Amazon secured long-term solar deals for Pacific Northwest centers

Microsoft restarted Three Mile Island nuclear reactor with Constellation Energy

This isn't ESG theater. This is survival.

THE NUCLEAR RENAISSANCE

Here's where it gets interesting. Renewables can't handle the load alone. Enter: Small Modular Reactors (SMRs).

Key players making moves:

GE Vernova + Hitachi building BWRX-300 reactors

NuScale Power securing government contracts

Tennessee Valley Authority partnering for grid expansion

Duke Energy and American Electric Power heavily invested

One analyst called it: "Where AI meets atomic."

THE BRIDGE TRADE OPPORTUNITY

These nuclear projects take years. Meanwhile, the existing grid is crumbling—over two-thirds of US transmission lines are beyond their intended lifespan.

That's why Hitachi Energy just acquired Blackstone's Shermco. Mission: keep the lights on while new capacity comes online.

STOCKS TO WATCH:
GEV - GE Vernova
CEG - Constellation Energy
SMR - NuScale Power
DUK - Duke Energy
AEP - American Electric Power
HTHIY - Hitachi

THE BOTTOM LINE

AI's next bottleneck isn't data. It's power.

While everyone chases chip stocks, the real winners might be the companies controlling energy production, transmission, and grid maintenance.

We are seeing some serious movement in Tesla stock tonight. TSLA surged over 1.6% in after-hours trading, hitting $410.50. If you are holding calls or long shares, you are waking up happy.

Here is the catalyst: President Trump just dropped details on his new tax plan, the "Big, Beautiful Bill." While the previous $7,500 EV credit was cut, this new bill introduces a massive deduction specifically for loan interest.

The Breakdown for Traders:

The Benefit: You can deduct up to $10,000 annually in interest on EV loans until 2028.

The Catch: The vehicle must have final assembly in the U.S. (Great for Tesla, tough for some competitors).

Income Limits: The full deduction applies to singles earning under $100k and couples under $200k.

The market reaction is interesting because this comes right after Ford and GM have been scaling back their EV production due to falling demand. Meanwhile, Trump explicitly said tonight, "You're so lucky I'm with you Elon," signaling a very strong continued alliance between the administration and Tesla.

The technicals were already looking good with a favorable price trend in the medium term, but this policy shift could be the fundamental push TSLA needs to break resistance.

QUESTION FOR MY TRADERS: If you had to pick just ONE of these to add to your portfolio TODAY, which would it be? Drop your answer below and WHY.

Here's what I'm seeing in the market right now...

Three blue-chip giants are trading at attractive entry points, and the numbers are telling a pretty compelling story. Let me break this down for you.

MICROSOFT (MSFT) - The Sleeping Giant
Yeah, I know what you're thinking - "Microsoft? That's boring." But hear me out.

Last quarter they pulled in $77.7 billion in revenue. 

That's an 18% jump year over year. They converted $30.8 billion of that straight to net income. These aren't the numbers of a company past its prime.

The stock has been flat since July while the broader market rallied. Why? Everyone's freaking out about their AI spending and the OpenAI partnership concerns. But here's the kicker - analysts still have this priced at $631.80. That's a 28% upside from current levels.

Think about it: Windows is on two-thirds of the world's computers. That kind of dominance doesn't just disappear. And that Windows monopoly? It funnels users straight into Bing, LinkedIn, and Azure. That's the moat everyone talks about but few truly appreciate.

COCA-COLA (KO) - The Dividend Machine
This one's been taking hits all year. Down from April highs while everything else recovered. Second quarter saw volume decline, tariff worries, the whole nine yards.

But let's get real for a second. This is a 139-year-old company that hasn't just survived - it's dominated. They're not just selling soda, they're selling a lifestyle. You see their logo on clothes, Christmas ornaments, home decor. That's brand power money can't buy.

Here's what caught my eye: 2.9% dividend yield. And get this - they've raised that dividend for 63 consecutive years. SIXTY-THREE. Through recessions, wars, pandemics, everything. That's the kind of consistency that builds generational wealth.

Current weakness? That's your entry point, not a red flag.

VISA (V) - The AI Play Nobody's Talking About

But here's what the market is missing: Visa's AI potential. Everyone's worried about competition and regulatory headwinds. Fair concerns. But nobody's pricing in what a data-rich payment processor can do with artificial intelligence.

We're talking AI-powered fraud detection in an industry drowning in fraud attempts. AI customer service. AI data analysis at a scale most companies can only dream about. This isn't AI for the sake of having AI - this is AI that actually moves the needle on the bottom line.

The stock pulled back from June highs. That pullback? That's opportunity.

These aren't some random picks. These are companies with decades of dominance, moats wider than the Grand Canyon, and current weakness that's giving us entry points we won't see for long.

But the stock sold off hard. Why? One reason: spending. Meta is pouring money into AI infrastructure and data centers. We’re talking 70 to 72 billion in 2025, and potentially 100 billion plus in 2026. Traders saw that number and panicked.

But go back in time. Meta did the same thing during the metaverse era. Heavy investments, stock drops, panic everywhere… and then the company got disciplined again and turned into a cash-flow machine. This pattern is not new. The chart on page 5 of the report shows Meta’s valuation bouncing off the low range it typically trades at during major fear cycles. 

History Says Now Is the Perfect…

Meta’s core ad business is still dominant.
AI is actually improving engagement and ad performance.
The stock is trading at one of its cheaper valuation points in years.

### Post #204: 2025-11-28 16:39 — `$INTC` `[Dd]`

**$INTC INTEL JUST JUMPED 6% - Here's Why This Could Be MASSIVE**

Ming-Chi Kuo just dropped a bombshell that sent INTC soaring today. The analyst who's rarely wrong about Apple says Intel is "significantly" more likely to become Apple's chip manufacturer.

Let me break down what's happening:

THE DEAL:

Intel could start making Apple's lowest-end M-series processors (the ones in MacBook Air and iPad Pro) using their advanced 18AP node technology. We're talking about 20 MILLION units per year.

THE TIMELINE:

Apple signed an NDA with Intel and already has the tech specs. They're waiting for Intel to release the final development kit in Q1 2026. If everything goes smoothly, Intel could start shipping chips by Q2-Q3 2027.

WHY THIS MATTERS:

First, it's huge for the "Made in USA" push under Trump's administration. Apple gets political points, Intel gets validation.

Second, and this is the big one - if Intel can land Apple, other tier-1 customers will follow. Kuo says "the worst may soon be over" for Intel's foundry business.

Third, this isn't just about one deal. Intel's 14A node and beyond could capture even MORE Apple orders down the line.

THE REALITY CHECK:

Intel still can't compete head-to-head with TSMC for several years. But this could be the inflection point where Intel stops bleeding and starts building credibility in the foundry space.

QUESTION FOR YOU:

Do you think Intel can actually execute on this? Or is this just analyst hype on a slow Friday before the holiday weekend?

While everyone's watching Tesla hit new highs, there's a contrarian opportunity brewing that could replicate TSLA's early success story.

The Tesla Playbook (that actually worked):

Remember how Tesla went from niche luxury (Roadster, Model S/X at 70K+) to mass market dominance? The Model 3 and Model Y under 50K changed everything. That sub-50K price point unlocked TENS OF MILLIONS of new buyers.

Rivian is at that EXACT inflection point:

Right now: Only R1S and R1T (luxury models)

Early 2026: Launching R2, R3, and R3X - ALL under 50K

This isn't speculation. Management just confirmed production is ON TRACK.

Here's where it gets interesting:

RIVN Price/Sales ratio: 3x

TSLA Price/Sales ratio: 15x

That's an 80% valuation discount for a company that's expected to grow TWICE as fast as Tesla in 2026 (29% vs 14.5%).

By end of 2026, Rivian could have as many affordable models as Tesla. Yet the market is pricing them like they're stuck in 2015.

The contrarian thesis:

Everyone's chasing AI and robotaxis. But the REAL money in EVs still comes from selling cars. Tesla proves this - most revenue still comes from Model 3/Y sales.

Rivian spent years building the infrastructure. Now they're ready to scale. The playbook is proven. The timing is right.

### Post #208: 2025-11-30 01:59 `[Discuss]`

**Why the Market Keeps Rising (Even When Your Stocks Don't)**

Ever notice how the market keeps grinding higher even when your favorite stocks are pulling back?

There's a hidden pattern most traders miss. It's called "The Great Mini Rotation" and it's been the engine behind this bull market's resilience.

Here's how it works:

AI and tech stocks blast higher for weeks or months. Then they pause or pull back as traders take profits. But instead of the whole market tanking, something interesting happens.

Money quietly rotates into the beaten-down sectors. Healthcare and biotech start breaking out. Energy stocks begin their run. The laggards become the leaders.

Then those new leaders get extended and pause. And guess what? Money rotates back into the original leaders or finds new undervalued areas. Rinse and repeat.

This is why the indexes can feel choppy day-to-day but still work their way to new highs. It's not one big wave lifting everything. It's a series of smaller waves taking turns.

The contrast with bear markets is striking. In bear markets, everything falls together. There's no rotation, only liquidation. But right now? Different sectors are taking turns driving the bus.

Think about 2025. AI stocks led early in the year. Then they cooled off and healthcare/biotech took over. Now we're seeing energy stocks break out as AI consolidates. The baton keeps passing.

This pattern has played out in every major bull market since the 1990s. Small, sequential leadership rotations keeping things dynamic and self-sustaining.

The biggest risk? Complacency. Assuming this rotation will always save the market. When a real macro shock hits, all sectors can fall together. But until then, this mini rotation is keeping the bull alive.

So what does this mean for your portfolio?

Stay diversified across sectors. Watch for improving relative strength in laggards. Don't chase every rotation, but be ready to lean into areas showing fresh momentum. The key is being in harmony with the market's rhythm.

The market isn't one organism anymore. It's an ecosystem where different species thrive at different times.

What sector do you think is setting up for the next rotation? Are you positioned for this pattern or fighting against it?

Source: Forbes - "The Market's Hidden Engine = The Great Mini Rotation" by Adam Sarhan (Nov 29, 2025)

Disclaimer: Not financial advice. This is market analysis for educational purposes. Always do your own research and consult with financial professionals before making investment decisions.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/RM20252029/bbza/why-the-market-keeps-rising-ev*

### Post #212: 2025-12-15 02:40 — `$OPEN` `[Dd]`

**Opendoor ($OPEN ) just dropped 8.3% on a story that traders love and hate at the same time: an AI pivot.**

The new CEO, Kaz Nejatian, is pushing Opendoor away from being a capital-heavy iBuyer and toward a software and AI-driven real estate platform. On paper, that’s the dream: less balance-sheet risk, more scalable margins, and a product story that markets are willing to pay up for.

But here’s the contrarian angle: “AI pivot” doesn’t automatically remove the two things that have historically haunted OPEN: housing-cycle sensitivity and balance-sheet stress. The article highlights a goal of breakeven on adjusted net income by end of 2026, while long-term debt sits around $973,000,000. That’s a real clock.

Valuation is another pressure point. The piece references a fair value estimate around 2.99 versus a share price shown near 6.56. That’s a big gap. Meanwhile, community fair value estimates range from 0.70 to 30.94, which tells you one thing: nobody agrees, so volatility is the feature, not the bug.

What this means for traders:
1) OPEN is trading like a narrative stock. If execution milestones slip, it can unwind fast.
2) If the AI-driven platform shows measurable progress (pricing accuracy, faster turns, lower costs), the market can re-rate quickly.
3) Meme/leveraged ETF flows can amplify both directions, which changes risk management more than it changes fundamentals.

Questions for you:
Do you think OPEN can realistically become a capital-light software business, or is real estate transaction risk impossible to “AI away”?
If you’re bullish, what’s the one KPI you want to see by mid-2026 that proves the pivot is working?
If you’re bearish, is the bigger issue the housing backdrop, the debt load, or simply trust after years of losses?

I’m watching this as a high-volatility setup where positioning and time horizon matter more than being “right” on the headline.

Source: Simply Wall St (Dec 14, 2025)

*Link: https://afterhour.com/RM20252029/bcIh/opendoor-dollaropen-just-dropp*

### Post #213: 2025-12-16 02:39 `[Dd]`

**Ford, ServiceNow, Luminar Technologies, AMC Robotics And iRobot: Why These 5 Stocks Are On Investors' Radars Today**

Monday's market wrapped with major indices in the red, but beneath the surface, five stocks told vastly different stories. Here's what traders need to know:

FORD: Killing the F-150 Lightning
Ford ended production of its current-gen electric pickup and pushed EV profitability targets to 2029. The stock barely budged, down just 0.66%. CEO Jim Farley is pivoting hard toward commercial vehicles and traditional trucks. Question is: are they abandoning EVs or just being realistic about demand?

SERVICENOW: The AI Darling Gets Humbled
Down 11.56% after a brutal KeyBanc downgrade to Underweight. The thesis? AI might actually hurt their seat-based revenue model, and Microsoft could eat their lunch by 2026. They're reportedly dropping 7 billion on cybersecurity firm Armis. Is this strategic growth or desperation?

LUMINAR: The Bankruptcy Nobody Saw Coming
LiDAR tech company cratered 60.82% after filing Chapter 11. They sold their semiconductor unit for 110 million, but it wasn't enough. The real story here is what it says about autonomous vehicle timelines. If LiDAR can't survive this long, what does that mean for the self-driving future?

AMC ROBOTICS: The SPAC That Actually Worked
Up 31.30% after completing their business combination. Former AlphaVest Acquisition just became a security tech play. SPACs have been disasters lately, so seeing one actually execute is noteworthy. But can they maintain momentum?

IROBOT: The Roomba Maker's Final Chapter
Down 72.69% and filing Chapter 11 after 35 years. Amazon walked away from a 1.7 billion acquisition due to regulatory issues, and now they're being bought by their Chinese manufacturer. Third quarter revenue down 25%, cash burned to almost nothing. This is what happens when you lose strategic optionality.

The bigger picture here is worth discussing. Ford is retreating from EVs. ServiceNow is facing AI disruption despite being an AI company. Luminar proves the self-driving timeline is still uncertain. And iRobot shows how quickly household names can collapse without strategic alternatives.

Which of these moves surprises you most? Are you buying Ford's pivot away from EVs, or do you think they're making a massive mistake? And is ServiceNow's weakness a buying opportunity or the start of a longer decline?

Drop your take below. I'm particularly curious what people think about the Luminar bankruptcy and what it signals for the autonomous vehicle sector.

Source: Benzinga Market Report, December 15, 2025
