### Post #216: 2025-12-29 07:04 — `$META, $IBKR, $WMT` `[Dd]`

**$META $WMT $IBKR - 2026 Key Stocks**

Three "Millionaire-Maker" stocks are catching attention right now, and they're not the usual suspects everyone's talking about.

Meta Platforms is pushing 26% revenue growth while quietly building out AI glasses that could become a serious revenue stream. With 3.54 billion daily active users and growing 8% year-over-year, their advertising moat keeps getting wider.

Interactive Brokers is the real sleeper here. Up 40% this year, they're adding 4.13 million accounts with a 32% growth rate. When retail traders are feeling bullish and margin loans jump 39% year-over-year, guess who wins? The brokers always collect their fees.

Walmart might surprise you. Everyone knows them as a retailer grinding on 3% margins, but their advertising business just grew 53% year-over-year. They're quietly building a high-margin revenue stream while using stores as distribution centers to crush e-commerce competitors.

Here's what I'm thinking: These aren't meme stocks or lottery tickets. They're businesses with actual moats, growing user bases, and multiple revenue streams. Meta dominates social, Interactive Brokers rides every market surge, and Walmart is transforming from low-margin retail to a hybrid ad platform.

The question is timing. Interactive Brokers thrives when markets are hot and retail participation is high. What happens when the music stops? Meta faces regulatory pressure globally. Walmart's ad business is still tiny compared to their retail operations.

Are these genuinely millionaire-makers, or are we late to the party? The article cites strong growth numbers, but every growth stock looks great until it doesn't.

What's your take? Would you buy any of these at current valuations, or are you waiting for a pullback?

Source: The Motley Fool, December 28, 2025

Disclaimer: Not financial advice. Do your own research. I may or may not hold positions in these stocks.

### Post #218: 2025-12-31 01:10 — `$MSFT, $F, $JD, $TSLA` `[News]`

**$MSFT $TSLA $JD $F Tech Giants Face Mixed Signals as Year Ends**

While everyone's watching the big names, some interesting patterns are emerging that could shape Q1 2026.

Microsoft just got a wake-up call. LG-backed Genspark AI is building a "superagent suite" that could fundamentally shift how we interact with work software. The pitch: white-collar workers spend 80% of their day shuffling between tools. Genspark wants to change that with AI that handles 30+ models simultaneously.

Here's the twist - Microsoft investors barely flinched. Stock ticked up despite the news. Wall Street's consensus: Strong Buy with 32 buys, 2 holds, and a $631 price target implying 29% upside.

Meanwhile, Tesla's having a rougher day. The company guided Q4 deliveries at 422,850 vehicles - well below the 445,000 analyst consensus and down 15% year-over-year. One major firm downgraded to equal-weight. Add in a key supplier slashing their battery deal, and suddenly that $1.5 trillion valuation at 17x sales is looking stretched.

The question everyone's asking: Can Tesla's pivot to autonomous vehicles and robotics justify the premium, or are we overpriced on promises?

Over in Chinese markets, XY Capital just dumped their entire JD.com position - 419,000 shares worth $13.7 million. The fund's sticking with Alibaba as their largest holding instead. JD's been stuck in neutral while Alibaba's taken off since 2024. The bet seems clear: they'd rather own the diversified tech conglomerate with cloud exposure than the pure e-commerce play.

And Ford's making waves by doubling down on CarPlay while GM exits the platform entirely. CFO Sherry House says engagement is up 20 points, and they're adding Gemini integration soon. Sometimes the winning move is just giving customers what they actually want.

What's interesting here is the divergence in strategies. Microsoft's staying flexible on AI models. Tesla's betting everything on autonomy. Ford's listening to customers while competitors chase proprietary systems.

Which approach wins in 2026? That's the real question.

The market's giving us mixed signals heading into year-end. Premium valuations everywhere, but not everyone's delivering premium growth. Something's got to give.

What's your take - are we in for a valuation reset, or can these companies grow into their multiples?

Source: TipRanks, Nasdaq, Motley Fool (Dec 30, 2025)

This is not financial advice. Markets can be unpredictable. Always do your own research and consider your risk tolerance before making investment decisions.

### Post #225: 2026-01-04 01:16 — `$NFLX, $META, $MSFT, $AAPL, $TSLA, $BABA` `[News]`

**$MSFT $TSLA $BABA $META $NFLX $AAPL TECH STOCKS WEEKLY RECAP: The Good, The Bad, and The Controversial**

Let's break down this week's biggest tech moves that have everyone talking.

THIEL'S MICROSOFT BET: GENIUS OR TOO LATE?

Peter Thiel just made waves selling his Nvidia and Tesla positions to go all-in on Microsoft. Yes, Microsoft - the stock that's already up 483,000% since its IPO. The Palantir co-founder is betting big on AI monetization through Azure and enterprise software. But here's the question: Is he late to the party, or does he see something we're missing?

Microsoft's AI copilots hit 150 million monthly users, and Azure is gaining cloud market share despite capacity constraints. The valuation sits at 33x earnings - pricey, but not crazy if those AI features keep driving growth.

TESLA'S DISAPPOINTING DELIVERIES

Speaking of Thiel selling Tesla - the EV maker just reported Q4 deliveries of 418,227 vehicles, down 16% year-over-year. This marks the second straight year of declining sales, and BYD just officially overtook them as the world's largest EV maker.

The silver lining? Tesla deployed a record 46.7 gigawatt hours of energy storage. Their robotaxi and energy businesses might be the real story here, not the cars. Wall Street seems to agree - the stock only dropped 2.6% despite missing estimates.

META'S SCAM AD SCANDAL

Meta is under fire after internal documents revealed they prioritized hiding scam ads from regulators rather than blocking them entirely. The financial impact? Potentially 10% of 2024 revenue (around $16 billion) could be tied to scam or banned products.

Yet Wall Street remains bullish with a $828.71 average price target - a 25% upside. The bet is that Meta can manage regulatory costs without derailing growth. But can they?

ALIBABA'S AI SURGE

While US tech deals with scandals, Alibaba is quietly dominating. The stock is up 72% this year as its cloud division posts 34% growth and its Qwen AI models surpass 600 million downloads - beating Meta's Llama in global usage.

The company is committing over $50 billion to expand cloud and AI infrastructure. Yes, it will hurt near-term profits, but analysts see 40% upside with a $205 average price target.

NETFLIX'S RISKY ACQUISITION

Netflix is buying Warner Bros. studio and streaming assets for $82.7 billion in a deal that could take 12-18 months to close. The market isn't loving it - the stock has been sliding on concerns about complexity and integration risks.

Management promises $2-3 billion in annual cost savings by year three, but at a 38x P/E ratio, there's little room for error. Is this strategic brilliance or unnecessary risk for a company that was already crushing it?

APPLE'S AI THREAT

OpenAI CEO Sam Altman openly sees Apple as his biggest long-term rival. He's pushing ChatGPT toward an "app-less" ecosystem that could bypass the App Store entirely. Meanwhile, some top analysts are questioning Apple's 31x valuation despite the company's strong position.

The big question: Can Apple's hardware-services ecosystem defend against AI-native platforms, or is the iPhone's dominance finally vulnerable?

THE BIG PICTURE

What connects all these stories? The AI gold rush is reshaping tech valuations, business models, and competitive dynamics. Traditional tech giants are betting billions on AI infrastructure while new AI-native companies are threatening established moats.

So here's my question for you: Which of these stories matters most for the next 12 months? And which stock would you buy today?

Source: Multiple financial news sources, TipRanks, 24/7 Wall St., Motley Fool, Nasdaq

**The AI Gold Rush: 50 Stocks Powering the Revolution**

Your roadmap to the companies literally building artificial intelligence** 💰⚡

While everyone’s talking about AI, these are the picks and shovels making it all possible:

## 🔥 **THE CHIP TITANS** (Foundry & Design)

*The brains behind the brains. Without these, AI doesn’t exist.*

**$NVDA** (Nvidia) - The undisputed GPU king. 90%+ of AI training runs on their chips.

**$AMD** (Advanced Micro Devices) - NVDA’s scrappiest competitor. Gaining ground fast with MI300 chips.

**$INTC** (Intel) - The legacy giant fighting back. Massive government backing for US chip manufacturing.

**$TSM** (Taiwan Semiconductor) - Makes chips for everyone. The world’s most important company you’ve never heard of.

**$AVGO** (Broadcom) - Custom AI chips for hyperscalers. Networking silicon for data centers.

**$ASML** (ASML Holding) - The company that makes the machines that make the chips. Literal monopoly on EUV lithography.

**$ARM** (Arm Holdings) - Powers every smartphone and now infiltrating data centers with energy-efficient designs.

⚡ **POWER PLAYERS** (Servers & Energy)

*AI is an energy monster. These companies feed the beast.*

**$VRT** (Vertiv Holdings) - Cooling systems for data centers. AI chips run HOT.

**$DELL** (Dell Technologies) - Building AI-optimized servers for enterprises everywhere.

**$EOSE** (Eos Energy) - Long-duration battery storage. Solving AI’s 24/7 power demands.

**$SLDP** (Solid Power) - Next-gen solid-state batteries. The future of energy storage.

**$FLNC** (Fluence Energy) - Grid-scale energy storage. Keeping AI infrastructure stable.

**$VST** (Vistra Energy) - Massive power generation portfolio. AI data centers need baseload power.

**$CEG** (Constellation Energy) - Nuclear power for clean, reliable AI infrastructure.

**$LEU** (Centrus Energy) - Nuclear fuel supplier. Clean energy is AI’s only sustainable path.

-----

## 🌐 **THE CLOUD KINGS** (Hyperscale & Networking)

*The backbone carrying AI to the world.*

**$GOOGL** (Alphabet) - DeepMind, TPUs, and the search engine feeding AI training data.

**$MSFT** (Microsoft) - OpenAI’s sugar daddy. Azure cloud printing money from AI workloads.

**$AMZN** (Amazon) - AWS dominates cloud infrastructure. Custom Trainium chips for AI training.

**$ORCL** (Oracle) - Database empire pivoting hard into AI cloud services.

**$CRDO** (Credo Technology) - High-speed connectivity chips for AI data centers.

**$ANET** (Arista Networks) - Networking switches moving AI data at light speed.

**$APH** (Amphenol) - Connectors and cables. The physical layer holding AI together.

**$CIEN** (Ciena) - Optical networking for massive data transfer between AI clusters.

-----

## 💾 **MEMORY MAKERS**

*AI’s insatiable appetite for storage and fast memory.*

**$SNDK** (SanDisk Corp) - High-performance storage solutions for data-intensive AI workloads.

**$STX** (Seagate Technology) - Mass storage leader. AI needs to remember EVERYTHING.

**$MU** (Micron Technology) - HBM (High Bandwidth Memory) for GPUs. Essential for AI performance.

**$WDC** (Western Digital) - Storage infrastructure from edge to data center.

**$PSTG** (Pure Storage) - All-flash arrays optimized for AI/ML pipelines.

-----

## 🔬 **THE DEEP TECH** (Optics, Manufacturing & Compute Mining)

*The specialists making cutting-edge AI infrastructure possible.*

**$LITE** (Lumentum) - Optical components for high-speed data transmission.

**$AAOI** (Applied Optoelectronics) - Fiber optic transceivers connecting AI clusters.

**$ALAB** (Astera Labs) - Connectivity solutions for AI infrastructure.

**$COHR** (Coherent) - Advanced materials and optics for next-gen chips.

**$SMTC** (Semtech) - Signal integrity chips for AI data centers.

**$IREN** (IREN Ltd) - Bitcoin mining pivoting to AI compute infrastructure.

**$WULF** (TeraWulf) - Zero-carbon crypto mining with AI compute potential.

**$CIFR** (Cipher Mining) - High-performance computing infrastructure.

**$HUT** (Hut 8) - Mining operations transitioning to AI workloads.

-----

## ☁️ **NEOCLOUD REVOLUTION**

*Next-generation distributed cloud infrastructure.*

**$NBIS** (NEBIUS) - AI-optimized cloud platform for developers.

**$CRWV** (CoreWeave) - GPU-specialized cloud. NVDA’s best friend in infrastructure.

**$APLD** (Applied Digital) - High-performance computing and AI hosting.

**$GLXY** (Galaxy Digital) - Digital infrastructure with AI compute pivot.

**$NUAI** (New Era Energy & Digital) - Emerging player in distributed AI infrastructure.

-----

## 🏭 **THE BUILDERS** (Manufacturing)

*Making the physical AI infrastructure real.*

**$CLS** (Celestica) - Contract manufacturing for networking and storage equipment.

**$FLEX** (Flex Ltd) - Building servers, storage, and networking hardware at scale.

**$FN** (Fabrinet) - Optical packaging and precision manufacturing for data centers.

**$SANM** (Sanmina) - End-to-end manufacturing solutions for tech infrastructure.

-----

## 💡 **THE BOTTOM LINE**

This isn’t just about software—it’s about **copper, silicon, electricity, and cooling systems**. AI needs physical infrastructure at a scale we’ve never seen before.

The companies above are literally building the future. Some will 10x. Some will fail. But together, they represent the biggest infrastructure build-out since the internet.

### Post #231: 2026-01-18 05:52 `[News]`

**Cathie Wood's Tesla Paradox: Bullish Talk, Bearish Trades**

Cathie Wood just dropped some interesting predictions that are splitting the trading community right down the middle.

While ARK has been trimming their Tesla position (sold $37.8M worth this week, $59M in December), Wood is doubling down on her long-term thesis. She's now eyeing SpaceX as the potential first trillion-dollar IPO, pointing to a fascinating new revenue stream: datacenters in space.

Here's what caught my attention:

Wood sees Tesla's robotaxi opportunity as a game-changer for margins. The shift from one-time EV sales to recurring revenue could fundamentally change how we value the company. She's sticking with her $2,600 price target by 2030 (that's an $8.19 trillion market cap from today's $437).

But here's the tension: if she's so bullish long-term, why the consistent selling? ARK's explanation is they're concentrating on highest-conviction plays during market volatility. Classic portfolio rebalancing or a signal of changing conviction?

The SpaceX angle is wild. Datacenters in space solves major problems: cooling, energy access, and latency for certain applications. If they nail this, the TAM could be enormous. Wood wouldn't be surprised if it becomes the first trillion-dollar IPO.

Tesla's dealing with a challenging EV sales environment right now. The stock is in consolidation mode with strong momentum indicators (69th percentile), but the autonomous driving narrative needs to start materializing into revenue.

What's your take? Is Wood playing 4D chess here, or are we seeing conviction crack in real-time? The gap between her public statements and her fund's actions is the most interesting part to me.

### Post #232: 2026-01-20 01:17 — `$AAPL` `[Dd]`

**$AAPL to $350 in 2026? Let's talk about what Wall Street isn't telling you.**

Warren Buffett's Berkshire has been sitting on Apple since 2016, and it's been one of his best moves ever. But here's where it gets interesting for us traders right now.

The bulls are pointing to Apple's track record. We're talking 48% gains in 2023, 30% in 2024, and a 942% run over the past decade. That's a 26.4% annual compound rate that crushes the S&P 500. Right now, the stock is sitting 9% below its peak at around $260.

But here's the reality check that most people are missing.

Apple's current P/E ratio is sitting at 34.7. Compare that to its 10-year average of 24.6, and you're looking at a premium valuation by any measure. The Street's consensus target is only $288, which implies about 11% upside, not the 35% needed to hit $350.

The catalyst everyone should be watching? iPhone sales. Q4 2025 showed 6% growth, and management is guiding for double-digit growth in Q1 fiscal 2026. Plus, that new Alphabet partnership bringing Gemini AI into Apple Intelligence could be the spark that drives upgrade cycles.

Here's my take: Apple is a quality company, no question. The ecosystem is unmatched, margins are strong at 30.6%, and they're printing money with $112 billion in net income. But expecting a 35% move in 11 months from an already expensive valuation? The author puts it at less than 50% probability, and I'm inclined to agree.

The real question for traders: are you playing the quality premium, or are you waiting for a better entry? Because at these levels, you're paying top dollar for a mature tech giant, not a high-growth disruptor.

What's your move here? Are you buying Apple at current levels, waiting for a pullback, or looking elsewhere for better risk-reward setups?

**Big Tech Earnings Week: The Market's Make-or-Break Moment $MSFT $META $TSLA $AAPL**

Next week could reshape the entire tech narrative. We're getting Q4 results from four of the Magnificent Seven all within 48 hours: Microsoft, Meta, and Tesla on Wednesday after the bell, followed by Apple on Thursday.

Here's what makes this different from typical earnings season: Wall Street is expecting an 8.2% earnings growth for the S&P 500, which would mark the 10th straight quarter of annual earnings growth. But here's the catch. Strip out the Magnificent Seven, and that growth rate drops to just 4.6%.

The real question isn't whether these companies beat estimates. It's whether they can justify their current valuations while spending tens of billions on AI infrastructure that hasn't fully monetized yet.

Microsoft's Azure growth will be under the microscope. Analysts expect 38% revenue growth in cloud, but the company's AI spending spree is intensifying. The street wants to see when those massive capital expenditures translate to actual returns.

Meta raised its 2025 capex forecast to 72 billion dollars, with even higher spending projected for 2026. The stock dropped 17% from its August peak after that announcement. This earnings call will test whether investors still believe in Zuckerberg's AI vision or if patience is running thin.

Tesla sits at almost 200 times estimated profits, making it the second most expensive stock in the S&P 500. Revenue is projected to rise 12% this year after an estimated 3% contraction in 2025. But Wall Street's average price target actually projects a 9% decline over the next 12 months. The robotaxi narrative needs to deliver.

Apple's momentum has slowed. The stock nearly matched its longest losing streak since 1991 before Friday's close. Revenue is expected to expand 9%, the fastest pace since 2021, but at 31 times earnings, it needs to prove the iPhone upgrade cycle and services growth can sustain the premium.

Volatility is back, but fundamentals remain intact. With Wall Street forecasts clustering around 7,500-8,000 for year-end 2026, there's potential upside—but selective, disciplined investing is the name of the game now.

The era of easy gains? Over.

Took a long hiatus from posting here. Honestly, most of my capital has been completely locked up in last year 2025 cash-secured puts (CSPs) and covered calls (CCs) from last year. Just playing the waiting game for them to break even so I can finally unwind those positions and free up that cash.

Because of that limitation, I've had to adapt. This year 2026, I'm playing almost entirely with margin. It’s a mix of a few degen plays, tactical CC/CSPs, and hunting for alpha in small-cap and mid-cap names.

Housekeeping. I’m moving everything out of Fidelity into Robinhood. Going forward it’s one account to monitor and report. Two big bags stay behind because they wouldn’t transfer one of them is MTPLF. Fidelity’s realized YTD is locked at $65,998.97 and carries forward in the combined number.

On unrealized. It’s gone. When the positions moved, the cost basis didn’t come with them the unrealized figure vanished in the transfer and I can’t calculate it anymore. I’m not going to reconstruct a number I can’t verify, so unrealized comes off the report. Realized is what I can prove, so realized is what I post.

Seven years off from options and apparently I came back without the lesson. Averaging down on a losing option is just paying more for less time I’m paying the exact theta I spend the rest of the week collecting. I know that. I did it anyway.

Working to get out of those and stay out, so the book is only running the thing that actually works:

Theta farming, momentum names in and out, small caps in and out.

That’s the mantra and I’m living by it. Everything else has been a tax.

### Post #256: 2026-09-03 00:35 — `$JD` `[Chart]`

**$JD at $27.90**

$39.9B market cap. $32.8B in cash and short-term investments at the end of Q2. Back the balance sheet out and you're paying roughly $23B for a business doing $191B in revenue.

The reason it's cheap: Aug 13 they posted the first revenue decline since the 2014 IPO, -2.9%. Stock fell 8%.

Same quarter, though : non-GAAP net income +20.8%, operating income swung from a loss to RMB 4.5B, EPS beat by 12%, and revenue actually beat consensus too.

The honest bear case, because it's a good one: ~91% of that operating improvement came from cutting food-delivery losses, not from growth. You only stop burning money once. And JD lost that war : ~16M daily orders vs Meituan's 80M.

What keeps me interested is the capital return. ~17% of shares retired since 2023. 3.6% dividend. ~6.9% total shareholder yield. At that pace revenue doesn't need to grow, it needs to stop falling.

Breaks if revenue hasn't turned by mid-2027.

Started writing these up properly : full piece here: https://rm20252029.substack.com/p/the-market-is-pricing-jdcom-like

Not financial advice.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/RM20252029/bvv0/dollarjd-at-dollar2790*
